[Euromonitor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euromonitor_International) estimates the [Sub-Saharan](27067.md) fashion [market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market_\(economics\)) to be worth \$31 billion, with Nigeria accounting for 15% of these \$31 billion.[^cite_note-402] Nigeria is not only known for their many fashion textiles and garment pieces that are secret to their culture. They also produced many fashion designers who have developed many techniques and businesses along the way.

### Sports

Main article: [Sports in Nigeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_in_Nigeria)



[Nigeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigeria_national_football_team) at the [2018 FIFA World Cup](13327177.md)

[Football](10568.md) is largely considered Nigeria's national sport, and the country has its own [professional football league](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigeria_Professional_Football_League). [Nigeria's national football team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigeria_national_football_team), known as the "Super Eagles", has played in the [FIFA World Cup](11370.md) on six occasions ([1994](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_FIFA_World_Cup), [1998](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998_FIFA_World_Cup), [2002](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_FIFA_World_Cup), [2010](168079.md), [2014](656933.md), and [2018](13327177.md)). In April 1994, the Super Eagles ranked fifth in the [FIFA World Rankings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Rankings), the highest ranking achieved by an African team. They won the [Africa Cup of Nations](250680.md) in [1980](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1980_African_Cup_of_Nations), [1994](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_African_Cup_of_Nations), and [2013](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Africa_Cup_of_Nations), and have also hosted both the U17 and U20 FIFA World Cup. They won the gold medal for football in the [1996 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1996_Summer_Olympics) (in which they beat Argentina) becoming the first African football team to win gold in Olympic football.[^cite_note-403]

Nigeria is also involved in other sports such as basketball, cricket and track and field.[^cite_note-404] [Nigeria's national basketball team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigeria_men's_national_basketball_team) made the headlines internationally when it became the first African team to beat the [United States men's national team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_men's_national_basketball_team).[^cite_note-405] In earlier years, Nigeria qualified for the [2012 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basketball_at_the_2012_Summer_Olympics) as it beat heavily favoured world elite teams such as [Greece](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greece_men's_national_basketball_team) and [Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuania_men's_national_basketball_team).[^cite_note-406] Nigeria has been home to numerous internationally recognised basketball players in the world's top leagues in America, Europe and Asia. These players include [Basketball Hall of Famer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naismith_Memorial_Basketball_Hall_of_Fame) [Hakeem Olajuwon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hakeem_Olajuwon), and later players in the [NBA](22093.md). The [Nigerian Premier League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigerian_Premier_League_\(basketball\)) has become one of the biggest and most-watched basketball competitions in Africa. The games have aired on *Kwese TV* and have averaged a viewership of over a million people.[^cite_note-407]

Nigeria made history by qualifying the first [bobsled](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bobsled) team for the [Winter Olympics](34069.md) from Africa when their women's two-person team qualified for [the bobsled competition at the XXIII Olympic Winter Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bobsleigh_at_the_2018_Winter_Olympics).[^cite_note-408] In the early 1990s, *[Scrabble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scrabble)* was made an official sport in Nigeria; by the end of 2017, there were around 4,000 players in more than 100 clubs in the country.[^cite_note-409] In 2018, the Nigerian Curling Federation was established to introduce a new sport to the country in order to make the game part of the curriculum at the elementary, high school, and university levels. At the [2019 World Mixed Doubles Curling Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_World_Mixed_Doubles_Curling_Championship) in Norway, Nigeria won their first international match beating France 8–5.[^cite_note-410]

Nigeria's women's and men's national teams in [beach volleyball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beach_volleyball) competed at the [2018–2020 CAVB Beach Volleyball Continental Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018%E2%80%932020_CAVB_Beach_Volleyball_Continental_Cup).[^cite_note-411] The country's U21 national teams qualified for the 2019 [FIVB Beach Volleyball U21 World Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIVB_Beach_Volleyball_U21_World_Championships).[^cite_note-412]

Nigeria is the birthplace of the sport [loofball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loofball).[^cite_note-413]
